However, the Bigg Building evidence shows there's an entrance to the hidden fifty-first floor on the roof (Evidence 2), which provides an alternative exit route from the roof that doesn't involve the elevator.

**Step 3:** The rule reveals the contradiction.

Rule 1's premise ("the elevator is the only exit") is violated by Evidence 2, which shows the hidden fifty-first floor entrance on the roof provides an alternative exit. This makes Lang's claim in Testimony 3 false.

## OBJECTIVE:
**FIND THE CONTRADICTION:** Which evidence and testimony contradict each other?

## CONSTRAINT:
**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** **Check if any generated rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
- Write your analysis first. Then the VERY LAST line must be exactly one JSON object like: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- The final line must contain only the JSON object with no leading or trailing text or whitespace (no labels, no markdown, no code fences). The response must end immediately at the closing brace """}""".

Examples:
- Good: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: **Step 5:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} extra
- Bad: ```json {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} ```

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at White's testimony, he claims:
- Testimony 8: "The victim ran to the left, and you gave chase!"
- Testimony 4: He saw "a spiky-haired man attacking a woman with long hair"

However, Evidence 9 (May Testimony) states: "The victim dodged an attack then ran to the right, but she was caught and struck."

This creates a direct spatial contradiction - White claims the victim ran to the left, while May's testimony indicates the victim ran to the right.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

Rule 1 applies here: "If XXX is to the left of YYY then YYY is to the right of XXX"

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Rule 1 reveals the contradiction because:
- White's testimony (Testimony 8) claims the victim moved to the left
- May's testimony (Evidence 9) claims the victim moved to the right
- According to Rule 1, if someone moves to the left, they cannot simultaneously move to the right. These are mutually exclusive directions.

**Step 4:** Not applicable - Rule 1 applies directly.

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 9, "testimony": 8}

PARSED RESULT:

**Step 2:** **Check if any generated rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
- Write your analysis first. Then the VERY LAST line must be exactly one JSON object like: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- The final line must contain only the JSON object with no leading or trailing text or whitespace (no labels, no markdown, no code fences). The response must end immediately at the closing brace """}""".

- Good: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: **Step 5:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} extra
- Bad: ```json {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} ```

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies.

Lang's testimony claims that after the president was pushed off the roof, Shaun and Gravelle hid the president's body inside the monster costume (Testimony 3). However, the Crime Scene Notes (Evidence 5) state that the body was found lying face-up on top of the dirt, with no dirt on the front of the body. This suggests the body was never inside the costume, as being inside a costume would likely result in dirt or debris on the front of the body, especially if the costume was on the ground or contained dirt (as Lang claims in Testimony 4).

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction.

Rule 2 states: "If XXX is inside YYY then YYY contains XXX." If the body were inside the monster costume, the costume would contain the body. However, the evidence shows the body was on top of the dirt, not inside anything.

**Step 3:** Rule 2 reveals the contradiction. Lang claims the body was inside the monster costume (implying the costume contains the body), but the Crime Scene Notes show the body was lying on top of the dirt, not inside any costume. This directly contradicts the spatial relationship implied by Lang's testimony.

**Step 4:** Not applicable, as Rule 2 applies.

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 5, "testimony": 3}

Rule 2 states: "If XXX is in KB Security then XXX is far from Lordly Tailor"

According to Rule 2, if Ron was at KB Security (as evidenced by the key card usage), he would be far from Lordly Tailor. ## CONSTRAINT:
**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 2:** **Check if any generated rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
- Write your analysis first. Then the VERY LAST line must be exactly one JSON object like: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- The final line must contain only the JSON object with no leading or trailing text or whitespace (no labels, no markdown, no code fences). The response must end immediately at the closing brace """}""".

Examples:
- Good: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: **Step 5:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} extra
- Bad: ```json {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} ```

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the crime scene photo (Evidence 9), we see:
- A glass of juice sits behind the victim on the dresser
- A full bottle of juice sits on the desk in front of the victim
- An open guitar case lies on the floor next to the victim
- Next to the guitar case are the remains of a smashed vase that has stained the carpet with water

In Andrews' testimony (Testimony 5), she states: "I was the one who knocked the flower vase over, where it fell onto the guitar case."

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Looking at the spatial rules:
- Rule 3: If XXX is next to the guitar case then XXX is on the floor
- Rule 15: If XXX is near the smashed vase then XXX is next to the guitar case

**Step 3:** Apply the rules to reveal the contradiction

From the crime scene photo, the smashed vase is on the floor next to the guitar case. According to Rule 15, since the vase is near the smashed vase (itself), it must be next to the guitar case. According to Rule 3, since the vase is next to the guitar case, it must be on the floor.

However, Andrews claims she "knocked the flower vase over, where it fell onto the guitar case." This implies the vase was originally on a higher surface (like the dresser or desk) and fell down onto the guitar case. But the crime scene shows the vase remains are on the floor next to the guitar case, not on top of it. If the vase had fallen onto the guitar case, we would expect to see vase fragments and water on the guitar case itself, not just on the floor next to it.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning shows that if the vase truly fell onto the guitar case as Andrews claims, there should be evidence of the vase being on the guitar case. Instead, the evidence shows the vase remains are on the floor next to the guitar case, suggesting the vase was already on the floor and was simply knocked over in place, not dropped from above onto the guitar case.

{"evidence": 9, "testimony": 5}

- Testimony 1: "He had a newspaper in his right hand"
- Testimony 4: "The little fidget picked up the cup with his free hand and took a sip"

This indicates the victim (Glen Elg) was holding a newspaper in his right hand and then used his "free hand" (which would be his left hand) to pick up and drink from the coffee cup.

However, examining the Crime Photo (Evidence 12) and Coffee Cup (Evidence 13):
- The coffee cup was knocked over and stained the table cloth
- The coffee cup had both the victim's and Maggey's fingerprints
- The cup had a floral handle to the right when facing it

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Rule 4 states: "If XXX is holding a newspaper in the right hand then XXX cannot be holding a coffee cup with the same hand"

**Step 3:** Apply the relevant rule to reveal the contradiction

According to Victor Kudo's testimony, Glen Elg was holding a newspaper in his right hand and used his "free hand" (left hand) to pick up the coffee cup. However, the spatial arrangement shown in the Crime Photo and Coffee Cup evidence suggests that the cup's floral handle was positioned to the right when facing it. This would make it extremely awkward and unnatural to pick up the cup with the left hand while holding a newspaper in the right hand, as the handle orientation would favor right-handed use.

Additionally, the fact that the cup was knocked over with coffee spilled suggests it was likely being used by someone right-handed, which contradicts Kudo's testimony that Elg used his "free hand" (left hand) while holding the newspaper in his right hand.

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Maya's testimony states that "The next day, when the bridge was finally fixed, she [Pearl] was in the spare prep room" (Testimony 2) and "That's when Mr. Godot arrived at the Inner Temple for the first time!" (Testimony 3).

However, according to the spatial rules, if Godot arrived at the Inner Temple for the first time, he must have crossed Dusky Bridge (Rule 3). But Maya also states that Pearl was "stuck on the Inner Temple side that night" (Testimony 0), which means Pearl couldn't cross Dusky Bridge until it was repaired (Rule 1).

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

Rule 3 applies: "If XXX arrives at the Inner Temple for the first time then XXX must have crossed Dusky Bridge"
Rule 1 applies: "If XXX is on the Inner Temple side then XXX cannot cross Dusky Bridge until it is repaired"

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

The contradiction lies in Maya's testimony that Godot arrived at the Inner Temple for the first time (Testimony 3) while Pearl was already in the spare prep room at the Inner Temple (Testimony 2). According to Rule 3, Godot must have crossed Dusky Bridge to reach the Inner Temple. However, Pearl being "stuck on the Inner Temple side" (Testimony 0) and being in the spare prep room (Testimony 2) creates a spatial impossibility - if Pearl was stuck on the Inner Temple side and couldn't cross the bridge until it was repaired, then Godot couldn't have arrived at the Inner Temple for the first time while Pearl was already there, as this would require both to be on the Inner Temple side before the bridge was repaired.

Rule 5: If XXX is lying under bedding then XXX must be inside a structure

**FIND THE CONTRADICTION:** Which evidence and testimony contradict each other?

## CONSTRAINT:
**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 2:** **Check if any generated rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:

- The final line must contain only the JSON object with no leading or trailing text or whitespace (no labels, no markdown, no code fences). The response must end immediately at the closing brace """}""".

Examples:
- Good: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: **Step 5:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} extra
- Bad: ```json {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} ```

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Butz testifies that he was in a shack 5 minutes walk from Eagle Bridge (Testimony 0), saw Dusky Bridge on fire at 10:45 (Testimony 2), and then immediately ran to check it out (Testimony 3). He claims he arrived at the bridge and then Nick showed up less than a minute later (Testimony 5).

However, the Weather Data (Evidence 7) shows that lightning struck Dusky Bridge at 10:45, which started the fire. Butz was in a shack 5 minutes away from the bridge. If he left immediately at 10:45 when he saw the fire, it would take him 5 minutes to reach the bridge, arriving at 10:50. But he claims Nick showed up "less than a minute later" after he arrived, which would place Nick's arrival around 10:50-10:51.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Rule 4: "If XXX is in a shack 5 minutes from YYY then XXX cannot reach YYY before 10:50 if leaving at 10:30"
Rule 12: "If XXX is in a shack 5 minutes from YYY and leaves at 10:30 then XXX arrives at YYY at 10:35"

These rules establish that a 5-minute travel time is fixed between the shack and the bridge.

**Step 3:** Apply the rules to reveal the contradiction

Using Rule 12's logic: If Butz was in a shack 5 minutes from Dusky Bridge and left at 10:45 when he saw the fire, he would arrive at 10:50. However, his testimony claims he arrived at the bridge and then Nick showed up "less than a minute later." This creates a contradiction because:

- Butz couldn't have arrived at the bridge before 10:50
- Yet he implies he arrived much sooner after seeing the fire at 10:45
- The timeline doesn't account for the full 5-minute travel time

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning shows that Butz's testimony about the timing of events contradicts the established 5-minute travel distance between his shack and Dusky Bridge.

{"evidence": 7, "testimony": 5}

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

From Testimony 3: "The doctor was standing in front of the bookshelves, absorbed in a book."
From Evidence 3 (Study Photo): "The victim is lying in the study next to floor-to-ceiling windows with clear glass. He is covered by books."

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Rule 7: "If XXX is absorbed in a book then XXX is facing the bookshelf"
Rule 9: "If XXX is standing in front of the bookshelves then XXX is between the bookshelves and the window"

**Step 3:** Apply the rules to reveal the contradiction

According to Rule 7 and Rule 9, if Dr. Buff was "absorbed in a book" while "standing in front of the bookshelves," he would be facing the bookshelves with his back to the window. However, the Study Photo (Evidence 3) shows the victim lying next to the windows, covered by books. This creates a spatial contradiction - if Dr. Buff was facing the bookshelves with his back to the window when struck, he would have fallen forward toward the bookshelves, not backward toward the windows where he is seen lying.

**Step 3:** Apply the rule to reveal the contradiction

However, Rule 8 indicates that if the victim (XXX) is found inside the broken lantern (YYY), then the victim must have been placed there before the lantern broke. This creates a spatial contradiction - the testimony suggests the victim fell into the lantern after being hit, but the rule implies the victim was already inside when the lantern broke.

**Step 4:** The contradiction is revealed through spatial reasoning

The spatial arrangement described in the testimonies (victim falling into lantern after being hit) conflicts with the spatial implication from the evidence (victim being inside the already-broken lantern).

Rule 10 states: "If XXX is holding a gun when found then XXX was holding the gun when the shot was fired." This rule directly applies to the contradiction.

**Step 3:** Explain how the rule reveals the contradiction

According to Rule 10, since Gina was found holding Windibank's gun (Evidence 12), this means Gina was holding the gun when the shot was fired. This directly contradicts Nash's testimony (Testimony 3) which claims Windibank was holding the gun at the time of the shooting.

Rule 12 states: "If XXX is strewn on the floor then XXX is at ground level"
Rule 15 states: "If XXX is sitting on YYY then XXX is on top of YYY"

**Step 3:** Apply the rules to reveal the contradiction

------------------------------

This creates a spatial contradiction - if the body was hidden inside a costume with dirt inside, the front of the body should have dirt on it, but the evidence shows no dirt on the front.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

Rule 2 states: "If XXX is inside YYY then YYY contains XXX." This rule applies here as the testimony claims the body (XXX) was inside the monster costume (YYY), which would mean the costume contains the body.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Rule 2 reveals the contradiction: If the body was inside the monster costume (as per Testimony 3), then the costume would contain the body. Given that there's dirt inside the costume (Testimony 4), the body would have dirt on its front surface. However, the Crime Scene Notes (Evidence 5) explicitly state there's "no dirt on the front of the body," which directly contradicts the spatial relationship implied by the body being inside a dirty costume.

**Step 4:** Not applicable - rules do apply.

Rule 8: "If XXX is facing left then XXX's back is to the right"

According to the photo (Evidence 6), Valerie (the victim) is facing left, which means her back is to the right (Rule 8). Fawles is standing to the left of Valerie, facing her. This means Fawles is facing Valerie's front, not her back. 

For Fawles to push Valerie "from behind" as stated in Testimony 3, he would need to be positioned behind her (to her right side, since she's facing left). However, the photo shows Fawles is to her left, facing her front. This directly contradicts the spatial requirement that someone pushed from behind must be facing away from the pusher (Rule 2).

- Testimony 3: "The killer, you, attacked her... but she dodged."

- Testimony 5: "Then you did her in with a single blow! Thwap!"

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

The contradiction is about the direction Mia ran after dodging the attack. White claims she ran to the left, while May claims she ran to the right. This is a direct spatial contradiction about left vs. right movement.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Rule 1 states: "If XXX is to the left of YYY then YYY is to the right of XXX"

This rule applies because if Mia ran to the left (as White claims), then from the perspective of her starting position, the door would be to her left. However, May claims she ran to the right, which would place the door to her right. These two statements cannot both be true simultaneously - if the door is to the left, it cannot also be to the right from the same reference point.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Not applicable - Rule 1 directly applies to this contradiction.

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies.

Looking at the testimonies, Simeon claims he was riding the balloon to the rooftop (Testimony 0) and saw the body double and Ms. Gravelle on the roof (Testimony 1). However, Security Camera Footage (Evidence 10) shows President Wang and Judge Gavèlle in front of the elevator on the roof of the Bigg Building, with no footage of the president riding the elevator down to leave.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction.

Rule 12 states: "If XXX is in the balloon then XXX is above the rooftop." This is relevant because Simeon claims he was in the balloon above the rooftop, which would give him a vantage point to see the roof.

**Step 3:** The contradiction arises because:
- Simeon claims he saw the body double (President Wang) and Ms. Gravelle on the roof from his position in the balloon above the rooftop (Rule 12 confirms this vantage point is possible)
- However, the Security Camera Footage (Evidence 10) shows President Wang and Judge Gavèlle arriving on the roof via the elevator, with no footage of the president leaving via the elevator
- This creates a spatial contradiction: if Simeon saw the president on the roof from his balloon, but the security footage shows no evidence of the president leaving the roof via the elevator, it suggests either the footage is incomplete or Simeon's testimony about what he saw is inaccurate

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning reveals that Simeon's testimony about seeing the president on the roof contradicts the security footage which shows no record of the president leaving the roof via the elevator, despite Simeon claiming the president entered the hatch to the 51st floor after firing at him.

- Rule 5: "If XXX is dry and clean then XXX was not on the ground outside" - The victim's coat in the trunk (Evidence 4) is dry and clean, indicating she was not on the ground outside when placed in the trunk

- This contradicts Testimony 4 which claims the scarf fell off during the attack on the bridge

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Melissa describes the killer carrying the victim's body from the bridge to the car (Testimony 1: "Then he carried her over to the car"). This implies the car was accessible from the bridge area.

However, Evidence 2 (Dusky Bridge Map) shows the bridge is 40 feet above Eagle River, with the parking lot to the west and the bridge broken midway towards the east. The sharp rock bed is under the bridge. This creates a spatial contradiction - if the car was in the parking lot (west of the bridge), carrying a body from the bridge to the car would require descending 40 feet from the bridge to the ground level.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Rule 15 states: "If XXX is 40 feet above YYY then YYY is 40 feet below XXX". This directly applies as the bridge is 40 feet above Eagle River (and by extension, the parking lot area).

**Step 3:** Explain how Rule 15 reveals the contradiction

Rule 15 confirms that the bridge is 40 feet above the ground level where the parking lot is located. Melissa's testimony describes the killer carrying the body "over to the car" after stabbing the victim on the bridge. However, this would require carrying the body down 40 feet from the bridge to the parking lot level, which contradicts the simple description of "carrying over" as if it were on the same level. The spatial layout makes this action much more complex than implied by the testimony.

**Step 4:** Additional spatial reasoning

The bridge's broken state (broken midway towards the east) and the 40-foot elevation difference create a significant barrier. The testimony oversimplifies the movement of the body from the bridge to the car, ignoring the substantial vertical distance that would need to be traversed.

This would mean Elise was facing TOWARD Maya, not away from her, which contradicts being stabbed in the back.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning shows that the testimony describes Elise facing the lantern (back to it) while being stabbed from behind, which is impossible if the attacker is in front of her.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at Kay's testimonies, specifically Testimony 3: "But... from under the cherry tree... I feel like... I saw something red." This indicates Kay was positioned under the cherry tree when she saw something red.

Looking at Testimony 4: "Umm... That's right, someone was walking towards me from the other side of that stand." This indicates the attacker came from the candy stand direction.

However, according to Evidence 8 (Kay's Recollections): "Kay remembers a person coming from beyond the concession stand and pushing her off of the building." This aligns with Testimony 4.

The contradiction appears when we consider the spatial arrangement. If Kay was under the cherry tree (left edge of viewing platform) and the attacker came from the candy stand (right edge of viewing platform), then the attacker would be approaching from Kay's right side. But this would mean Kay was facing toward the candy stand when pushed.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Rule 4: "If XXX is beneath the cherry tree then XXX is on the left edge of the viewing platform"
Rule 5: "If XXX is near the candy stand then XXX is on the right edge of the viewing platform"
Rule 15: "If XXX is pushed from behind then XXX was facing away from the attacker"

**Step 3:** Apply the rules to reveal the contradiction

According to Rule 4, Kay was on the left edge (beneath the cherry tree). According to Rule 5, the attacker came from the right edge (candy stand). If the attacker pushed Kay from the candy stand (right edge) while Kay was at the cherry tree (left edge), this would be a push from the side, not from behind. However, Kay's recollection in Evidence 8 suggests she was pushed from behind, which would require her to be facing away from the candy stand. But if she saw the red object "from under the cherry tree" and then saw someone approaching from the candy stand, she would have been facing toward the candy stand, making a push from behind impossible.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning shows that Kay's position under the cherry tree (left edge) and the attacker's approach from the candy stand (right edge) creates a contradiction with the claim that she was pushed from behind while seeing the attacker approach.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Andistan'dhin claims in Testimony 3 that he saw Ahlbi in the treasure room "through the window." However, Evidence 5 (Notice) states that "All of the hallway shutters will remain shut for today's Dance of Devotion." This creates a spatial contradiction because if the shutters are closed, Andistan'dhin cannot see through the windows into the hallway, and therefore cannot see into the treasure room.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Rule 10 states: "If XXX is in the hallway and the shutters are closed then XXX cannot see through the windows"
Rule 12 states: "If XXX is in the hallway and the shutters are closed then XXX cannot see into Andistan'dhin's Chamber"

**Step 3:** Apply the rules to reveal the contradiction

Since the shutters are closed (per Evidence 5), and the treasure room can only be seen from Andistan'dhin's Chamber through windows in the hallway (per Evidence 6), Rule 10 and Rule 12 indicate that visibility through these windows is blocked. Therefore, Andistan'dhin's claim in Testimony 3 that he saw Ahlbi through the window is impossible given that the shutters were closed.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Rule 11 applies: "If XXX jams a door from the inside then XXX must be inside when jamming occurs."

**Step 3:** Explain how Rule 11 reveals the contradiction

According to Rule 11, whoever jammed the door (using the Broken Prop Sword) must have been inside the room when jamming occurred. However, Lance's testimony states he was left alone in the room with his hands cuffed (Testimony 3), which would prevent him from jamming the door. This suggests either:
1. Someone else was present in the room to jam the door (contradicting Testimony 3 that he was left alone), or
2. Lance could jam the door despite being cuffed (which seems physically impossible)

**Step 4:** Not applicable as Rule 11 directly applies

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 12, "testimony": 3}

This directly contradicts Fairplay's testimony (Testimony 4) which claims that if anyone else had been in the carriage, they "all would have seen" them. The obscured face in the photograph proves that visibility inside the carriage was not complete, making Fairplay's absolute claim impossible.
